Mermaid diagrams (they render on GitHub). They match the [paper](../paper/main.tex) and the
[project page](../project-page/index.html).

## 1. Paradigms as plugins

```mermaid
flowchart TB
  Y["configs/&lt;variant&gt;/small.yaml<br/>inherits: ../base.yaml"] --> R{"registries<br/>variant  collate  loss  sampler  metric"}
  R --> AR["models_autoregressive"]
  R --> MD["models_diffusion"]
  R --> BD["models_block_diffusion"]
  R --> FM["models_flow_matching"]
  R --> MTP["models_mtp (stub)"]
  AR & MD & BD & FM & MTP --> CORE["shared: core backbones  WikiText-2 + GPT-2 windows  trainer  evaluator  inference  GIF visualiser"]
```

## 2. Training objectives

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  X["x (128 GPT-2 tokens)"] --> ARc["AR: causal mask<br/>labels shifted by 1"] --> ARl["CE(next token)"]
  X --> MDc["Masked diffusion: t ~ U(,1)<br/>mask each token w.p. t"] --> MDl["CE on masked  1/t"]
  X --> BDc["Block diffusion: t_k per 16-token block<br/>input = [clean ; noisy], block-causal mask"] --> BDl["CE on masked noisy  1/t_k"]
  X --> FMc["Flow matching: t ~ U(0,1)<br/>mask w.p. 1t"] --> FMl["CE on masked  1/(1t)<br/>(= MD with s = 1t)"]
```

## 3. Block diffusion: attention and decoding

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  subgraph Attention["who attends to whom"]
    direction TB
    c["clean block k"] -->|"blocks  k"| cc["clean"]
    n["noisy block k"] -->|"blocks &lt; k"| cc
    n -->|"block k only"| nn["noisy"]
  end
  subgraph Decode["sampling"]
    direction TB
    p["prompt blocks fixed"] --> b1["block j: all [MASK]"]
    b1 --> s1["S steps, t: 1  0<br/>reveal w.p. (t  s)/t"]
    s1 --> f["freeze block j"] --> b2["block j + 1"]
  end
```

## 4. Shared backbone

```mermaid
flowchart TB
  IDS["token ids"] --> EMB["token embedding (tied)<br/>+ learned positions (GPT / DiT)"]
  T["diffusion time t"] --> TE["sinusoidal  MLP  t_emb"]
  EMB --> ST{"model.arch"}
  ST -->|transformer| G["GPTStack: pre-LN, MHA, MLP"]
  ST -->|lgt| L["LGTStack: RMSNorm, GQA, 5 local : 1 global,<br/>RoPE  10 / 10, GeGLU"]
  ST -->|dit| D["DiTStack: AdaLN-Zero"]
  TE -. "scale/shift norms" .-> L
  TE -. "AdaLN" .-> D
  G & L & D --> H["LM head  logits (B, S, 50,258)"]
```

## Sizes (measured, `configs/base.yaml`)

| Paradigm | Backbone | Params |
|---|---|---|
| AR | GPT, MLP, MHA | 47.7M |
| Diffusion / block diffusion / flow matching | LGT, GeGLU, GQA n_kv = 2, time-conditioned | 63.2M |

# Block flow matching (`variant: block_flow_matching`)

Block flow matching relates to `flow_matching` the way `block_diffusion` relates to `diffusion`.
It generates **autoregressively across blocks** and uses **discrete flow matching inside each
block**. The code is in
[`src/hale_llm/models/models_block_flow_matching/`](../src/hale_llm/models/models_block_flow_matching/).

| File | Content |
|---|---|
| `path.py` | `MaskingPath`: the `(t)` schedule, the rate `'/(1-)` and the exact Euler reveal probability; `corrupt_blocks` draws one `t` per block |
| `model.py` | `BlockFlowMatchingLM` (`@register_variant`) and its batch collator |
| `loss.py` | `@register_loss`: masked-token cross-entropy weighted by `'(t)/(1-(t))` |
| `sample.py` | `@register_sampler`: generates one block at a time, integrating the flow from `t: 0  1` inside each block |
| `metrics.py` | `masked_accuracy` |

## Probability path

A token is data with probability `(t)` and the mask token otherwise:

```
p_t(x | x) = (t) (x) + (1  (t)) ([MASK]),   (0) = 0, (1) = 1
```

There are two schedules, set with `model.fm_schedule`:

- `linear`: ` = t`;
- `cosine`: ` = 1  cos(t/2)`. It reveals few tokens early and most of them near `t = 1`.

The rate `'(t)/(1  (t))` plays two roles. It is the weight of the cross-entropy at time `t`
(`1/(1  t)` on the linear path, as in `flow_matching`). It also sets how fast masked tokens
are revealed while sampling.

## Training

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  X1["x (clean)"] --> C["clean stream, t = 1"]
  X1 --> N["per block b: t_b ~ U[0, 1)<br/>mask each token w.p. 1  (t_b)"]
  N --> XT["noised stream x_t, t = t_b"]
  C --> BB["backbone on [x ; x_t]<br/>block_causal mask"]
  XT --> BB
  BB --> L["CE on masked positions<br/> '(t)/(1(t))"]
```

The training sequence has two streams:

- The backbone reads `[x_clean ; x_t]`, which doubles the length.
- With the `block_causal` mask, noised block `b` sees the clean blocks before it and itself.
  The clean stream sits at the end of the flow (`t = 1`).
- Every block gets its own `t`, so one forward pass trains all blocks in parallel, each on its
  clean prefix.

## Sampling

The sampler fills blocks from left to right; blocks made only of prompt tokens are skipped. Each
block starts fully masked at `t = 0`. At each of the `sample.sampling_steps` Euler steps from `t`
to `t'`:

1. The model predicts `x` for every position in the block.
2. Each masked position takes its prediction with probability
   `((t')  (t)) / (1  (t))`. This is exact on the path for any step size.

A final greedy pass fills any token still masked at `t = 1`.

The output covers the blocks up to `prompt + max_new_tokens`. Its length is bounded by
`max_length`, as in `block_diffusion`. History frames carry `{block, num_blocks, block_size}`,
so the training GIFs highlight the active block.

## Config

```yaml
variant: block_flow_matching
model:
  attn_type: block_causal   # required
  use_time_cond: true       # required
  block_size: 16            # divides max_length
  fm_schedule: linear       # linear | cosine
sample:
  sampling_steps: 16        # Euler steps per block
  eps: 0.001                # t is kept below 1  eps
```

There are two configs: `configs/block_flow_matching/small.yaml` (LGT, linear path) and
`configs/block_flow_matching/nano.yaml` (cosine path). Any `arch` works, including `looped`.

```bash
hale-llm-train --config configs/block_flow_matching/small.yaml --viz
hale-llm-sample --config configs/block_flow_matching/small.yaml --prompt "The meaning of life is"
```

## How it compares

| | `flow_matching` | `block_diffusion` | `block_flow_matching` |
|---|---|---|---|
| Across positions | all at once | autoregressive over blocks | autoregressive over blocks |
| Inside a block | masking flow `t: 0  1` | absorbing diffusion `t: 1  0` | masking flow `t: 0  1` |
| Loss weight | `1/(1  t)` | `1/t` | `'(t)/(1  (t))` |
| Variable length | no (fixed canvas) | yes (blocks) | yes (blocks) |

## References

- Campbell et al., *Generative Flows on Discrete State-Spaces*, ICML 2024, arXiv:2402.04997.
- Gat et al., *Discrete Flow Matching*, NeurIPS 2024, arXiv:2407.15595.
- Arriola et al., *Block Diffusion: Interpolating Between Autoregressive and Diffusion Language
  Models*, ICLR 2025, arXiv:2503.09573.

# HALE-LLM blog

- [2026-09-28  Same model, four ways to write: AR, masked diffusion, block diffusion and flow matching](blog/2026-09-28-four-ways-to-write.md)

---

Linked in initial post

        .        .

Six months ago, I built an autoregressive LLM from scratch. No fine-tuning. No API. Just a laptop, stubbornness, and a growing caffeine addiction.

It worked. Slowly.

One token at a time. Always waiting. Always linear.

Building it taught me the raw mechanics of prediction, the hunger for compute, and the beauty of watching a model learn to speak. I understood its soulthe token-by-token march to coherence.

Then I stumbled into the next frontier: #BlockDiffusion.

Different math. Weird architecture. Total paradigm flip.

It felt like learning to walk again. You're not predicting the next word anymoreyou're orchestrating a symphony of simultaneous transformations. The math is different. The intuition is different. The failures are spectacularly different.

I broke the sampler first. The parallel denoising steps kept diverging because I'd misweighted the transition kernelevery run spat out linguistic gibberish. I rebuilt it from the ground up, stripped it back to first principles, and watched it collapse again. For three days, I almost reverted to autoregressive out of sheer shame.

Heads-down, I kept pushing. Tweaking. Testing. Failing forward.

Then, on the fourth morning, it clicked.

Now? 1 token  16 tokens.

Why does this matter? Because speed isn't bragging rightsit's viability. It's the difference between a research paper and a tool that actually works in the real world. It's about making generative AI less of a resource hog.

Code's live. Speed's real. Still rough around the edgesbut it works.

I've pushed it to our repo: basaanithanaveenkumar/Hale-LLM

This is still the middle of the story. The model isn't perfect. There are kinks to iron out and a mountain of optimization ahead.

But I'm learning that the best part of this journey isn't the destinationit's the constant, humbling act of reinvention.

To everyone out there grinding through a new paradigm: keep going. The payoff is in the breakthrough, not the blueprint.

#MachineLearning #AI #GenerativeAI #LLM #BlockDiffusion #OpenSource #InferenceOptimization #BuildInPublic #DevLife

# Configuration reference

Every run is a YAML file under `configs/`. Variant files **inherit** [`configs/base.yaml`](../configs/base.yaml) and override only what they need. Unknown keys are rejected (`extra: forbid` in the Pydantic schema).

```yaml
inherits: ../base.yaml
variant: block_diffusion
model:
  arch: lgt
```

Schema lives in [`src/hale_llm/config/sections/`](../src/hale_llm/config/sections/) (one file per YAML block). [`RunConfig`](../src/hale_llm/config/run.py) composes them; [`load_config`](../src/hale_llm/config/load.py) handles YAML inheritance.

Pass a file to the CLI:

```bash
uv run hale-llm-train --config configs/block_diffusion/small.yaml --no-resume --viz
```

CLI flags (`--epochs`, `--steps`, `--resume`, `--prompt`, `--experiment`) override YAML after load.

---

## Inheritance

1. Load the file you pass (`--config`).
2. If it has `inherits:`, load that parent (and its parent, one extra hop).
3. Deep-merge: nested dicts merge; scalars in the child **replace** the parent.

`inherits` is not a `RunConfig` field; it is stripped during merge.

Put shared size, data, and logging in `base.yaml`. Put paradigm-specific `variant`, `model.attn_type`, `model.arch`, and `model.block_size` in `configs/<variant>/small.yaml`.

---

## Top-level

| Key | Type | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `variant` | string, **required** |  | Which registered model/loss/sampler to use: `autoregressive`, `diffusion`, `block_diffusion`, `flow_matching`, `block_flow_matching`, `looped`, `mtp`. Must match a `@register_variant` name. |
| `device` | `mps` \| `cuda` \| `cpu` \| `null` | `null` (auto) | Training device. `null` lets the runtime pick. Example: `device: mps` on Apple Silicon. |
| `model` | object | see below | Architecture. |
| `train` | object | see below | Optimizer loop. |
| `data` | object | see below | Dataset and tokenizer. |
| `sample` | object | see below | Generation. |
| `eval` | object | see below | Validation metrics. |
| `viz` | object | see below | Unmasking GIFs. |
| `logging` | object | see below | Loguru + TensorBoard. |
| `experiment` | object | see below | Run folders under `data/experiments/`. |

---

## `model`

Shared width/depth for every variant. `arch` chooses the **block stack**; `attn_type` chooses the **mask**.

### Size

| Key | Type | Default (schema / `base.yaml`) | How to use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `d_model` | int | `128` / `384` | Residual stream width. Must be divisible by `n_heads`. Larger  more capacity and VRAM. |
| `n_heads` | int | `4` / `8` | Query heads. |
| `n_layers` | int | `4` / `16` | Transformer / DiT / LGT depth. |
| `d_ff` | int | `512` / `1536` | FFN inner size (per expert if MoE). Typical ~4 `d_model`. |
| `dropout` | float | `0` / `0.1` | Attention and FFN dropout. |
| `max_length` | int | `64` / `128` | Tokens per training window and position table. Sequence length must be divisible by `block_size` for block diffusion. |

### Backbone (`arch`)

| Value | Stack | Typical use |
|---|---|---|
| `transformer` or `default` | GPT-style pre-norm + LayerNorm + learned positions | Autoregressive, MTP |
| `lgt` | Local-global transformer: RMSNorm sandwich, GQA, dual RoPE, 5:1 sliding-window / global, GeGLU | Diffusion, block diffusion, flow matching |
| `dit` | AdaLN-Zero DiT blocks (scale, shift, residual gates) | Same denoisers, alternative to LGT |
| `looped` | One weight-tied block (`n_layers` layers) applied `loop.n_loops` times: depth embedding, input injection, optional ACT halting / prelude / coda / scratch tokens ([looped.md](looped.md)) | Any variant |

```yaml
model:
  arch: lgt    # or dit, or transformer
```

Swap without changing variant code: all models call `build_backbone(...)`.

### Attention mask (`attn_type`)

| Value | Meaning | Variant |
|---|---|---|
| `causal` | Token *i* attends to * i* | `autoregressive`, `mtp`, `looped` |
| `bidirectional` | Full self-attention | `diffusion`, `flow_matching` |
| `block_causal` | AR across blocks, bidirectional inside the current block (clean + noised streams) | `block_diffusion`, `block_flow_matching` |

### Attention implementation

| Key | Type | Default | How to use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `attn_impl` | `mha` \| `gqa` \| `mqa` | `mha` | `mha` = PyTorch `MultiheadAttention`. `gqa` = grouped-query (set `n_kv_heads`). `mqa` = one KV head. LGT forces GQA internally. |
| `n_kv_heads` | int \| `null` | `null` | KV heads for GQA. Must divide `n_heads`. `null`  `n_heads/4` for GQA, or LGT local `n_heads/2` and global `n_heads/8`. Example: `n_heads: 8`, `n_kv_heads: 2`. |

### FFN

| Key | Type | Default | How to use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ffn_type` | `mlp` \| `geglu` \| `moe` | `mlp` | `mlp` = LinearGELULinear. `geglu` = gated GELU (LGT default if you leave `mlp` on `arch: lgt`). `moe` = noisy top-k experts + shared experts. |
| `moe_num_experts` | int | `4` | Routed experts (ignored unless `ffn_type: moe`). |
| `moe_top_k` | int | `2` | Experts active per token. Must be ` moe_num_experts`. |
| `moe_num_shared` | int | `1` | Experts applied to every token. |

VRAM: MoE multiplies FFN parameters. Prefer `geglu` on a laptop; use `moe` when you want sparsity.

### Time, blocks, MTP

| Key | Type | Default | How to use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `use_time_cond` | bool | `false` | AdaLN (transformer/LGT) or DiT timestep embedding. Set `true` for diffusion / block diffusion / flow matching. Leave `false` for AR. |
| `block_size` | int \| `null` | `null` | Tokens per block for `attn_type: block_causal`. Required for block diffusion / block flow matching. `max_length` must be a multiple (e.g. `128` and `16`). |
| `n_mtp_heads` | int | `2` | Extra next-token heads for the MTP stub. Unused by other variants. |
| `fm_schedule` | `linear` \| `cosine` | `linear` | `block_flow_matching` masking path `(t)`: `t` or `1  cos(t/2)` ([guide](block_flow_matching.md)). |

### LGT-only (ignored by `transformer` / `dit`)

| Key | Type | Default | How to use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sliding_window` | int | `512` | Local layers attend at most this many tokens away. If ` max_length`, local  full attention; global layers still use p-RoPE. |
| `local_global_ratio` | int | `5` | Five local layers then one global (`(i+1) % 6 == 0`). |
| `rope_theta_local` | float | `10000` | RoPE base on local layers. |
| `rope_theta_global` | float | `1e6` | RoPE base on global layers. |
| `p_rope` | float | `0.25` | Fraction of head dim rotated on **global** layers (p-RoPE). Local layers rotate the full head. |
| `qk_norm` | bool | `false` | RMSNorm on Q and K. LGT always applies QK-Norm. |

---

### Looped (`model.loop`, only with `arch: looped`)

`n_layers` is the shared core block; compute depth = `prelude_layers + n_loops * n_layers + coda_layers`. Full guide: [looped.md](looped.md).

| Key | Type | Default | How to use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `n_loops` | int | `4` | Passes through the shared block (training maximum, inference default). |
| `prelude_layers`, `coda_layers` | int | `0`, `0` | Distinct layers before / after the loop. |
| `depth_embedding` | `sinusoidal` \| `learned` \| `none` | `sinusoidal` | Loop-step coordinate added to each pass. Only `sinusoidal` can run more loops than `n_loops`. |
| `input_injection` | `add` \| `concat` \| `none` | `add` | Re-feed the loop input every pass (`h = 0`); `none` starts from `h = e`. |
| `sandwich_norm` | bool | `false` | Post-norms in the core layers; bounded state for long loops. |
| `halting` | `none` \| `act` | `none` | `act` = Adaptive Computation Time per token. |
| `act_threshold`, `act_init_bias` | float | `0.99`, `1.0` | ACT `1 - eps`; initial halting logit (negative = ponder longer at init). |
| `ponder_weight` | float | `0.01` | ``: ponder loss `  mean(N + R)` added by the trainer. |
| `min_loops` | int \| `null` | `null` | Training: sample `T ~ U{min_loops..n_loops}` each step. |
| `bptt_loops` | int \| `null` | `null` | Training: backprop through the last `k` passes only. |
| `exit_tolerance` | float \| `null` | `null` | Inference: stop when the per-token relative change falls below it. |
| `scratch_tokens` | int | `0` | Learned memory slots carried across loops (causal masks: slots read only each other). |

## `train`

| Key | Type | Default (schema / `base.yaml`) | How to use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `epochs` | int \| `null` | `null` / `2000` | Full passes over the loader. If set, **wins** over `steps`. CLI: `--epochs`. |
| `steps` | int \| `null` | `300` / `null` | Optimizer steps if `epochs` is `null`. CLI: `--steps` (ignored when `--epochs` is set). |
| `lr` | float | `1e-3` / `3e-4` | AdamW learning rate. |
| `batch_size` | int | `8` / `32` | Sequences per step. `drop_last=True`; if the split is smaller than this, the loader is empty. Lower on MPS (block diffusion uses `28`). |
| `weight_decay` | float | `0` / `0.01` | AdamW decay. |
| `log_every` | int | `50` / `100` | Log loss / lr / grad every N steps. |
| `grad_clip` | float | `1.0` | Global grad-norm clip. `0` or very large disables in practice if you change code; keep `1.0`. |
| `seed` | int | `0` | `torch.manual_seed`. |
| `checkpoint_path` | string | `checkpoints/last.pt` | Overwritten by the experiment layout to `.../checkpoints/last.pt`. |
| `checkpoint_every_epoch` | bool | `true` | Save `last.pt` at epoch end. |
| `resume` | bool | `true` / `false` | Load `last.pt` if present. CLI `--resume` / `--no-resume`. `--no-resume` starts a new timestamped run. |
| `loss_type` | `ce` \| `focal` \| `label_smoothing` | `ce` | Registered token loss. Add more with `@register_token_loss`. |
| `focal_gamma` | float | `2.0` | Focal focusing (`loss_type: focal`). |
| `focal_alpha` | float | `1.0` | Focal class weight. |
| `label_smoothing` | float | `0.1` | Smoothing when `loss_type: label_smoothing`. |

**Schedule:** `epochs: 2000` and `steps: null`  2000 epochs. `epochs: null` and `steps: 1000`  enough epochs to cover 1000 steps.

---

## `data`

`null` on sizes means **use the entire Hugging Face split**, not no data.

| Key | Type | Default | How to use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `source` | `huggingface` \| `overfit` | `huggingface` | Hub corpus vs repeated overfit string. `overfit_text` set also enables overfit. |
| `dataset` | string | `Salesforce/wikitext` | Hub id: `load_dataset(dataset, subset, split=...)`. |
| `subset` | string \| `null` | `wikitext-2-raw-v1` | Config name. WikiText-103: `wikitext-103-raw-v1`. `null` if the dataset has no configs. |
| `train_split` | string | `train` | Split name on the Hub. |
| `val_split` | string | `validation` | Some datasets use `test` or `valid`. |
| `text_field` | string | `text` | Column with raw text. |
| `cache_dir` | string \| `null` | `null` | Hugging Face cache. `null`  default `~/.cache/huggingface`. |
| `tokenizer_name` | string | `gpt2` | `AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained`. Must match a Hub tokenizer. |
| `train_size` | int \| `null` | `null` | Keep the first N Hub **rows** (not windows). `null` = all. Example: `2048` for a smoke run. |
| `val_size` | int \| `null` | `null` | Same for validation. |
| `stride_words` | int | `10` | Sliding window step in **words**. `1` = dense overlap (more windows, slower). Larger = fewer windows. |
| `overfit_text` | string \| `null` | `null` | If set (or `source: overfit`), skip the Hub and pack this string. |
| `n_overfit_copies` | int | `64` | Repeated copies so `batch_size` and `drop_last` still work. |
| `add_special_tokens` | bool | `false` | Reserved for tokenizer encode; sliding windows currently encode without specials. |

### Recipes

Full WikiText-2 (current default):

```yaml
data:
  source: huggingface
  dataset: Salesforce/wikitext
  subset: wikitext-2-raw-v1
  train_size: null
  val_size: null
  stride_words: 10
```

Subset for debugging:

```yaml
data:
  train_size: 512
  val_size: 128
  stride_words: 20
```

WikiText-103:

```yaml
data:
  subset: wikitext-103-raw-v1
```

Overfit:

```yaml
data:
  source: overfit
  overfit_text: "hello world this is a tiny overfit sentence"
  n_overfit_copies: 64
```

---

## `sample`

Used by `hale-llm-sample`, `hale-llm-viz`, and training GIFs (viz can shorten `sampling_steps`).

| Key | Type | Default (schema / `base.yaml`) | How to use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `max_new_tokens` | int | `32` / `64` | Tokens generated after the prompt. |
| `temperature` | float | `1.0` | Softmax temperature. Lower  sharper. |
| `sampling_steps` | int | `32` | Denoising / flow steps. Ignored by greedy AR decode except as unused. More steps  slower, usually cleaner diffusion. |
| `eps` | float | `1e-3` / `0.001` | Minimum noise level (avoid `t=0` in diffusion/flow). |
| `prompt` | string | `"hello"` / `"The meaning of life is"` | Default prompt. CLI `--prompt` overrides. Viz uses this only if `viz.prompt_source: config`. |

---

## `eval`

| Key | Type | Default | How to use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `metrics` | list of strings | `loss`, `perplexity`, `bits_per_token`, `token_accuracy`, `masked_accuracy` | Names from the metric registry. LLM metrics live in `hale_llm.metrics.llm` (`topk_accuracy` is optional). Unknown names error. CLI `hale-llm-eval --metrics loss,perplexity`. |
| `every_n_epochs` | int \| `null` | `1` / `10` | Run eval during training. `null` skips periodic eval. Block diffusion `small.yaml` uses `1`. |
| `max_batches` | int \| `null` | `null` | Cap val batches. `null` = full val loader. Set e.g. `20` to speed training. |

---

## `viz`

Training GIFs go to the experiment `viz/` folder (layout overrides `output_dir`).

| Key | Type | Default (schema / `base.yaml`) | How to use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `enabled` | bool | `false` / `true` | Master switch. CLI `--viz` / `--no-viz`. |
| `every_n_steps` | int \| `null` | `500` / `50` | GIF every N optimizer steps. `null` disables step-based GIFs. Cheap: `500`. Frequent: `50`. |
| `every_n_epochs` | int \| `null` | `null` | Extra GIF at epoch boundaries. |
| `sampling_steps` | int \| `null` | `8` | Denoise steps **inside training GIFs** (keep small). `hale-llm-viz` uses `sample.sampling_steps`. |
| `max_new_tokens` | int \| `null` | `null` | Override generation length for GIFs. `null`  `sample.max_new_tokens`. |
| `prompt_source` | `dataset` \| `config` | `dataset` | `dataset` = prefix from validation paragraphs. `config` = `sample.prompt`. `--prompt` forces config. |
| `prompt_words` | int | `8` | Words taken from a validation passage. |
| `output_dir` | string | `data/viz` | Unused when experiments are on; files land in the run `viz/`. |

## `logging`

| Key | Type | Default | How to use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `level` | `DEBUG` \| `INFO` \| `WARNING` \| `ERROR` | `INFO` | Loguru level. CLI `--log-level`. |
| `log_file` | string \| `null` | `logs/hale_llm.log` | Redirected to `.../logs/train.log` under the experiment. `null` = stderr only. |
| `tensorboard` | bool | `true` | Write scalars. |
| `tensorboard_dir` | string | `runs` | Redirected to the run `tb/`. `tensorboard --logdir data/experiments`. |

Scalars: `train/loss`, `train/lr`, `train/grad_norm`, `eval/*`.

## `experiment`

| Key | Type | Default | How to use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `enabled` | bool | `true` | If `true`, checkpoints/logs/tb/viz live under `root`. Tests set `false`. |
| `root` | string | `data/experiments` | Parent directory. |
| `name` | string \| `null` | `null` | Folder name. `null`  `<variant>_<YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS>`. CLI `--experiment NAME` reuses a folder. |
| `source_yaml` | string \| `null` | set at load | Path of the YAML you passed; do not set by hand. |

Layout:

```
data/experiments/<variant>/<variant>_<stamp>/
  config.yaml
  configs/
  checkpoints/last.pt
  logs/train.log
  tb/
  viz/
latest -> <that folder>
```

## Variant YAML cheat sheet

| File | `variant` | `arch` | `attn_type` | Extra |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `configs/autoregressive/small.yaml` | `autoregressive` | `transformer` (from base) | `causal` | `use_time_cond: false` |
| `configs/diffusion/small.yaml` | `diffusion` | `lgt` | `bidirectional` | `use_time_cond: true` |
| `configs/block_diffusion/small.yaml` | `block_diffusion` | `lgt` | `block_causal` | `block_size: 16`, `use_time_cond: true` |
| `configs/flow_matching/small.yaml` | `flow_matching` | `lgt` | `bidirectional` | `use_time_cond: true` |
| `configs/block_flow_matching/small.yaml` | `block_flow_matching` | `lgt` | `block_causal` | `block_size: 16`, `fm_schedule: linear`, `use_time_cond: true` ([guide](block_flow_matching.md)) |
| `configs/mtp/small.yaml` | `mtp` | `transformer` | `causal` | `n_mtp_heads: 5` (stub) |
| `configs/looped/{nano,small,act,recurrent_depth}.yaml` | `looped` | `looped` | `causal` | `model.loop` ([looped.md](looped.md)) |
| `configs/looped/{diffusion,block_diffusion}_nano.yaml` | `diffusion`, `block_diffusion` | `looped` | per paradigm | looped backbone under a denoiser |

To try DiT on block diffusion, only change:

```yaml
# configs/block_diffusion/small.yaml
model:
  arch: dit
```

## CLI vs YAML

| Command | Required | Useful flags |
|---|---|---|
| `hale-llm-train` | `--config` | `--no-resume` new run; `--resume` / `--experiment NAME`; `--epochs` / `--steps`; `--viz` |
| `hale-llm-sample` | `--config` | `--experiment`, `--prompt`, `--checkpoint`, `--num-loops` (looped) |
| `hale-llm-eval` | `--config` | `--experiment`, `--metrics a,b`, `--num-loops` (looped) |
| `hale-llm-viz` | `--config` | `--experiment`, `--prompt`, `--output` |

Sample / eval / viz attach to `latest` unless `--experiment` is set. They do not create a new run folder.

# Core library

`hale_llm.core` is a reusable decoder toolkit. Language-model training (losses, samplers, variants) lives beside it; **stacks and components do not depend on those**.

```
core/
  components/     attention, FFN, RMSNorm, TransformerLayer, DiTBlock
  transformers/   GPTStack, LGTStack, DiTStack    hidden states (B, S, D)
  backbones/      GPT / LGT / DiT token LMs       ids  logits
  registry.py     variant/loss/sampler plugins (this repo)
```

## VLM or world model

Supply your own embeddings (image patches, latents, text). Run a stack:

```python
from hale_llm.core.transformers import LGTStack, GPTStack, DiTStack

stack = LGTStack(d_model=512, n_heads=8, n_layers=12, d_ff=2048, use_time_cond=False)
hidden = stack(tokens, attn_mask=mask, positions=positions)  # (B, S, D)
```

`GPTStack` is a plain pre-norm decoder. `LGTStack` is local/global + dual RoPE. `DiTStack` is AdaLN-Zero (pass `t_emb`).

## Token language model

```python
from hale_llm.core.backbones import build_backbone, GPTBackbone, LGTBackbone, DiTBackbone

model = build_backbone(vocab_size, cfg.model)          # arch from YAML
logits = model(token_ids, t=t, attention_mask=mask)    # (B, S, V)
```

Register another stack with `@register_backbone("my_arch")` in `core/backbones/factory.py` (open/closed).

## Components

```python
from hale_llm.core.components import (
    build_attention, build_ffn, TransformerLayer, DiTBlock, RMSNorm,
)
```

1. wiki dataset 

tokenizer :
RDTvlokip/ag-bpe -- maximum efficeient and better semantic meaning

# Deploying HaloDiffusionLLM models

Every registered variant can be exported to NVIDIA (TensorRT 10/11, Jetson, TensorRT-RTX),
Apple Silicon (MLX), AMD (MIGraphX on Instinct/Radeon, Ryzen AI NPU through Quark + Vitis AI)
and Qualcomm (Snapdragon NPU through AI Hub or ExecuTorch QNN). The heavy lifting lives in
HaleBlocks' `hale_core.deploy` framework (stages, registries, config inheritance, parity
checks, the torch  MLX bridge); this repository contributes one **adapter per variant** and
the configs that bind variants to targets.

```bash
pip install -e ".[deploy]"            # + deploy-nvidia | deploy-apple | deploy-amd | deploy-qualcomm
hale-llm-deploy env                   # which backends this machine can run
hale-llm-deploy list                  # stages, runtimes, and the hale_llm.* adapters
hale-llm-deploy run configs/deploy/diffusion/tensorrt_fp16.yaml \
    --cfg-options adapter.checkpoint=data/checkpoints/last.pt
```

Artifacts land in `work_dirs/deploy/<name>/<stage>/` together with `config.yaml` (the merged
config), `manifest.json` (every artifact with size and sha256) and `report.json`.

## Architecture

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    subgraph repo["HaloDiffusionLLM (this repo)"]
        RC["run config YAML<br/>configs/&lt;variant&gt;/*.yaml"] --> RES[RunConfigResolver]
        CK[(trainer checkpoint<br/>last.pt)] --> AD
        RES --> AD["hale_llm.*Adapter<br/>(one per variant)"]
        COR[CalibrationCorpus<br/>offline tokenizer] --> AD
        DC["configs/deploy/&lt;variant&gt;/&lt;target&gt;.yaml"]
    end
    subgraph hb["HaleBlocks hale_core.deploy"]
        PK["packaged targets<br/>pkg://hale_core.deploy/configs"] -->|_base_| DC
        DC --> RUN[DeployRunner]
        AD -->|ADAPTERS child scope hale_llm| RUN
        RUN --> P[Pipeline]
        P --> ONNX[OnnxExportStage]
        P --> MLXC[MLXConvertStage]
        P --> AIH[QAIHubCompileStage]
        P --> ET[ExecuTorchQNNStage]
        ONNX --> MO[ModelOpt AutoCast / PTQ] --> TRT[TensorRTEngineStage]
        ONNX --> MIG[MIGraphXCompileStage]
        ONNX --> QK[QuarkQuantizeStage] --> PKG[OnnxRuntimePackageStage]
        ONNX --> PAR{{RuntimeParityStage}}
        TRT --> PAR
        MLXC --> PAR
        MIG --> PAR
    end
    TRT --> E1[".engine<br/>H100  RTX  Jetson Orin"]
    MLXC --> E2[".mlxfn + safetensors<br/>MacBook / Mac Studio"]
    MIG --> E3[".mxr<br/>MI300X  Radeon"]
    PKG --> E4["ONNX + deployment.json<br/>Ryzen AI NPU (Vitis AI EP)"]
    AIH --> E5[".dlc / .tflite / .bin<br/>Snapdragon 8 Elite"]
    ET --> E6[".pte<br/>Android (QNN HTP)"]
```

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant U as hale-llm-deploy
    participant R as DeployRunner
    participant A as hale_llm adapter
    participant P as Pipeline
    participant S as Stage (e.g. TensorRTEngineStage)
    U->>R: run diffusion/tensorrt_fp16.yaml
    R->>R: resolve _base_ chain, import custom_imports (hale_llm.deploy)
    R->>A: ADAPTERS.build({type: hale_llm.MaskedDiffusionLMAdapter, ...})
    R->>P: validate data flow (requires / provides) before any work
    loop every stage
        P->>A: model / signature / calibration batches (lazy, cached)
        P->>S: run(ctx)
        S-->>P: artifacts (onnx, engine, ...)
        P->>P: store artifacts, record status + timing
    end
    P-->>U: report.json, manifest.json, exit code
```

## Variants and their export contracts

All token inputs are `int64` ids in `[0, vocab)`; `vocab = 50258` for the GPT-2 tokenizer plus
the `[MASK]` token (read from the checkpoint when one is given). `batch  [1, max_batch]`.

| variant | adapter (`hale_llm.`) | inputs | outputs | seq | what the app runs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| autoregressive | `AutoregressiveLMAdapter` | `input_ids [B,T]` | `logits [B,T,V]` | dynamic `[1, max_length]` | prefill per generated token |
| looped | `LoopedLMAdapter` | `input_ids` | `logits` | dynamic | loop unrolled `n_loops` times |
| mtp | `MultiTokenPredictionLMAdapter` | `input_ids` | `logits`, `draft_logits_1..K-1` | dynamic | self-speculative decoding, one model |
| diffusion | `MaskedDiffusionLMAdapter` | `x_t [B,T]`, `t [B]` | `logits` | static `max_length` | `sampling_steps` denoiser calls |
| flow_matching | `FlowMatchingLMAdapter` | `x_t`, `t [B]` | `logits` | static | Euler steps `t: 0  1` |
| block_diffusion | `BlockDiffusionLMAdapter` | `x_clean`, `x_noised`, `t_noised [B,T]` | `logits` | static | per-block denoising |
| block_flow_matching | `BlockFlowMatchingLMAdapter` | `x_clean`, `x_t`, `t [B,T]` | `logits` | static | per-block Euler steps |

The samplers (unmasking schedules, Euler steps, speculative verification) stay in the
application: the exported artifact is the network, called by the same loop as
`hale_llm.inference`.

### Targets per variant

Every variant has the nine target configs below (`configs/deploy/<variant>/`), each inheriting a
packaged HaleBlocks target:

| target | vendor / hardware | pipeline |
|---|---|---|
| `tensorrt_fp16` | NVIDIA H100/H200/B200/L40S | ONNX  ORT parity  ModelOpt AutoCast (TRT 11)  engine  engine parity |
| `tensorrt_int8_modelopt` | NVIDIA | ONNX  ModelOpt INT8 Q/DQ (real-text calibration)  strongly typed engine |
| `jetson_orin_dla_fp16` | Jetson Orin (JetPack 6, TRT 10) | ONNX  engine with DLA core 0 + GPU fallback (build on the device) |
| `mlx_fp16` / `mlx_int4` | MacBook / Mac Studio | torch.export  ATenMLX bridge  `.mlxfn` + safetensors  MLX parity |
| `migraphx_fp16` | AMD MI300X / Radeon (ROCm 7) | ONNX  MIGraphX `.mxr` (AOT)  parity |
| `ryzen_ai_npu_a16w8` | Ryzen AI 300 NPU | ONNX  Quark A16W8  ORT + VitisAI EP bundle |
| `aihub_qnn_dlc_w8a16` | Snapdragon 8 Elite (Galaxy S25) | AI Hub compile (QNN DLC, W8A16) + profile + on-device parity |
| `executorch_qnn_16a8w` | Snapdragon 8 Gen 3 | ExecuTorch + QNN HTP delegate, PT2E 16a8w  `.pte` |

### Verified on this repo (Apple Silicon, tiny CI models)

`configs/deploy/ci/` runs every variant end to end (`tests/integration/test_deploy_pipelines.py`):

* ONNX (dynamo exporter, TorchScript fallback)  ONNX Runtime: all 7 variants pass at the
  min / opt / max shapes (max abs error < 1e-3, fp32).
* MLX fp32 through the ATen  MLX bridge: all 7 variants pass (max abs error < 1e-4).

TensorRT, MIGraphX, Quark, AI Hub and ExecuTorch QNN need their SDK / hardware; their configs
are validated (they build valid pipelines) and the stages are unit-tested in HaleBlocks against
SDK fakes.

### Not supported (and why)

* **TensorRT-LLM / mlx-lm / mlx-vlm** - they serve Hugging Face architectures; Hale variants are
  custom (bidirectional denoisers, block-causal two-stream inputs, MTP heads), so the adapters
  return no HF checkpoint. The ONNX / MLX graph paths above are the deployment route.
* **ACT halting (looped)** - per-token early exit is data-dependent control flow; exporters
  trace a fixed graph. Deploy with `halting: none` (the adapter rejects ACT configs explicitly).
* **Dynamic sequence length for LGT-based diffusion configs** - the LGT backbone guards
  positions with a Python `min(seq, max_length)` that `torch.export` cannot prove for a
  symbolic length. The diffusion-family samplers use a fixed canvas, so these adapters export a
  static `max_length` (set `dynamic_seq: true` for `arch: transformer` models).
* **KV cache** - exported causal graphs recompute the prefix; an incremental-decoding graph
  would need an explicit cache-in / cache-out signature (future work).

## Calibration

Quantizing targets use `AdapterCalibrationSource`: each adapter tokenizes a small built-in
corpus with the run's tokenizer (`local_files_only` - a deployment job never downloads) and
applies the variant's own corruption (masking with a sampled `t`, per-block times), so the
quantizer sees the activations the model sees in production. Without a cached tokenizer the
pipeline falls back to random ids.

## Research notes (October 2026)

* **TensorRT 11** is strongly typed only: `kFP16/kINT8/...` builder flags and the INT8 calibrator
  are removed; precision comes from the ONNX graph via **ModelOpt AutoCast** (mixed precision)
  or ModelOpt Q/DQ quantization
  ([migration guide](https://docs.nvidia.com/deeplearning/tensorrt/latest/api/migration/tensorrt-10x-to-11x-c-api-patterns.html),
  [ModelOpt](https://nvidia.github.io/Model-Optimizer/reference/0_changelog.html)). The stage
  feature-detects TensorRT 10 vs 11.
* **TensorRT-LLM 1.2** removed the TensorRT engine backend and `trtllm-build`; the PyTorch backend
  is the only one ([release notes](https://nvidia.github.io/TensorRT-LLM/release-notes.html)).
* **MLX** exports traced functions to `.mlxfn` with `mx.export_function` / `mx.exporter`
  (several shapes per file), importable from Python, C++ and Swift
  ([export guide](https://www.mintlify.com/ml-explore/mlx/guides/export)).
* **ROCm 7.1+** drops ONNX Runtime's ROCm EP; MIGraphX (directly or as the MIGraphX EP) is the
  AMD GPU path ([ORT ROCm EP](https://onnxruntime.ai/docs/execution-providers/ROCm-ExecutionProvider.html)).
* **Ryzen AI** NPUs run INT8 / BF16 ONNX through the Vitis AI EP; **Quark** A16W8 is the
  recommended transformer preset ([Vitis AI EP](https://onnxruntime.ai/docs/execution-providers/Vitis-AI-ExecutionProvider.html),
  [Quark A16W8](https://quark.docs.amd.com/release-0.11/supported_accelerators/ryzenai/tutorial_a8w8_and_a16w8_quantize.html)).
* **Qualcomm AI Hub** deprecated `qnn_context_binary` (Mar 2026) and `precompiled_qnn_onnx`
  (May 2026) as compile targets; context binaries come from `submit_compile_and_link_jobs`
  ([release notes](https://workbench.aihub.qualcomm.com/docs/hub/release_notes.html),
  [API](https://workbench.aihub.qualcomm.com/docs/hub/api.html)).
* **ExecuTorch QNN**: `to_edge_transform_and_lower_to_qnn` + `QnnQuantizer` (PT2E), QNN SDK 2.37
  recommended ([backend docs](https://docs.pytorch.org/executorch/1.2/_sources/backends-qualcomm.md.txt)).

## Vendored SDK sources

`ext_pkgs/` holds shallow git submodules of the vendor SDKs (TensorRT, TensorRT-LLM,
Model-Optimizer, Torch-TensorRT, MLX, MIGraphX, Quark, Ryzen AI SW, AI Hub models, ExecuTorch)
for reading samples and pinning versions; see `ext_pkgs/README.md`. Fetch them with
`git submodule update --init --depth 1 ext_pkgs/<name>` (do not recurse).

# Ray Train and WebDataset

Both features come from HaleBlocks (`hale_core.distributed.ray`, `hale_core.data.webdataset`;
design and internals in HaleBlocks' `docs/distributed_training.md`). This page covers what
`hale-llm` adds. Install the extras with `uv sync --extra distributed`, or `--extra ray` /
`--extra webdataset` for one of them.

## Ray Train: `train.ray`

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  CLI["hale-llm-train --config "] --> T["hale_llm.training.train(cfg)"]
  T -->|"train.ray.enabled"| L["hale_core launch_on_ray  TorchTrainer"]
  L --> W["N workers: train(cfg) with parallel_strategy=ddp<br/>DistributedSampler per rank  RayReporter each epoch"]
  W --> R["RayRunResult: metrics + checkpoint path"]
  T -->|"disabled"| LOCAL["Trainer(cfg).fit() as before"]
```

* `hale_llm.training.train` checks `cfg.train.ray.enabled`. On the driver it launches the same
  function on `num_workers` Ray workers. Each worker trains with DDP: Ray creates the process
  group, which `setup_distributed` picks up.
* Each rank reads its own shard of the token windows (`DistributedSampler`, reshuffled every
  epoch with `set_epoch`).
* At the end of every epoch all ranks report `epoch`, `global_step`, `train/epoch_loss` and any
  `eval/*` metrics, and rank 0 attaches the checkpoint directory. After a worker failure Ray
  restarts the group and the checkpoint is restored into `train.checkpoint_path`, so
  `train.resume: true` continues from it. `max_failures` sets how many times this can happen.
* On the driver `train()` returns a `RayRunResult` (`metrics`, `checkpoint_path`) instead of
  `(model, tokenizer, losses)`.

| key | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `enabled` | `false` | launch through Ray Train |
| `address` | `null` | `null` = local Ray instance, `"auto"` / `"ray://host:10001"` = join a cluster |
| `num_workers` | `1` | data-parallel replicas |
| `use_gpu` / `resources_per_worker` | `false` / `{}` | GPU per worker / e.g. `{CPU: 8, GPU: 1}` |
| `storage_path` | `ray_results` | where reported checkpoints and results are stored |
| `max_failures` / `num_to_keep` | `0` / `2` | fault tolerance / checkpoints kept |
| `backend` | `auto` | NCCL with GPUs, Gloo on CPU |

## WebDataset: `data.webdataset`

With `enabled: true`, `make_dataloader` builds the `HuggingFaceTextDataset` (download +
tokenisation into windows) **only if** no valid shards exist. It writes the windows to
`<root>/<dataset>-<subset>-<split>-<fingerprint>/shard-*.tar` and then streams them. The
fingerprint hashes everything that changes the windows: dataset, subset, split, size, text
field, tokenizer, `max_length`, `stride_words` and `add_special_tokens`. Changing any of these
writes a new shard set, and the old ones are kept. Batches are identical to the non-WebDataset
loader (tested). Overfit mode (`data.overfit_text`) is a debugging path and is not converted.

| key | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `enabled` | `false` | convert + stream |
| `root` | `data/webdataset` | shard root |
| `samples_per_shard` | `1000` | windows per tar file (write  ranks  loader workers shards) |
| `convert_workers` | `0` | parallel reads while converting |
| `shuffle_buffer` | `1000` | in-memory sample shuffle for training |
| `resample` | `auto` | equal-length epochs on every rank when several ranks train |
| `overwrite` | `false` | force re-conversion |

# Library layout

Install the core blocks library in any project:

```bash
uv add "hale-blocks @ git+https://github.com/basaanithanaveenkumar/HaleBlocks.git"
```

Install the full training stack:

```bash
uv add "hale-llm @ git+https://github.com/basaanithanaveenkumar/HaloDiffusionLLM.git"
```

`hale_llm` is split so other projects (VLM, world models, custom LMs) can import pieces without the training CLI.

```
hale_llm/
  core/           components, transformer stacks, token backbones
  losses/         registered token losses (ce, focal, label_smoothing, kl)
  metrics/llm/    loss, perplexity, bits/token, accuracy, top-k
  dataloader/     tokenizer, windows, HF / overfit sources, collate registry
  evaluation/     Evaluator over a loader
  inference/      session, generate, encode_prompt
  models/         paradigm plugins (AR, diffusion, )
```

## Losses

```python
from hale_llm.losses import token_nll, register_token_loss, TOKEN_LOSSES

nll = token_nll(logits, targets, loss_type="focal", focal_gamma=2.0, reduction="mean")
nll = token_nll(logits, targets, loss_type="label_smoothing", label_smoothing=0.1)

@register_token_loss("my_loss")
def my_loss(logits, targets, *, ignore_index=-100, **kwargs):
    ...
```

YAML: `train.loss_type: ce | focal | label_smoothing` plus `focal_gamma`, `focal_alpha`, `label_smoothing`.

Variant losses (`@register_loss("diffusion")`) still live under `models_*`; they call `model_token_nll`.

## Metrics

```python
from hale_llm.metrics.llm import LossMetric, PerplexityMetric, TokenAccuracyMetric, TopKAccuracyMetric
from hale_llm.core.registry import register_metric

@register_metric("my_metric")
class MyMetric:
    ...
```

YAML `eval.metrics`: `loss`, `perplexity`, `bits_per_token`, `token_accuracy`, `topk_accuracy`, plus variant `masked_accuracy`.

## Data

```python
from hale_llm.dataloader import DataModule, sliding_windows, setup_tokenizer, register_collate
```

Sources: `dataloader/sources/huggingface.py`, `dataloader/sources/overfit.py`.

## Eval / inference

```python
from hale_llm.evaluation import Evaluator, evaluate
from hale_llm.inference import load_session, generate, encode_prompt
```

Core stacks (no tokenizer): see [core.md](core.md).

## Registries

Two primitives in `hale_core.registry` (from **hale-blocks**):

| Type | Use when | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| `NamedRegistry` | one config name maps to one implementation | model, loss, sampler, optimizer, config, logger, trainer, token loss, backbone, attention, ffn |
| `VariantRegistry` | one config name may have variant-specific implementations | metrics |

## `hale_core` layout (HaleBlocks)

```text
registry/    plugin infrastructure
config/      RunConfig + YAML loading
logging/     loguru + experiment backends
training/    Trainer, schedule, distributed
nn/
  layers/    attention, FFN, norm, embeddings
  stacks/    GPT, LGT, DiT depth modules
  backbones/ token models
  losses/    token losses
  optim/     optimizer wrappers
runtime/     checkpoint, tensors, device
```

Training plugins in `hale_llm` register via `@register_variant`, `@register_loss`,  Import `hale_llm.models` so registration runs before lookup.

`hale_llm.core` is a thin compatibility namespace over `hale_core` (install **`hale-blocks`**).

# Experiment Logging

HaloDiffusionLLM supports multiple experiment tracking backends for comprehensive monitoring and visualization of training runs.

## Supported Backends

### 1. TensorBoard (Local)
TensorBoard provides local visualization of training metrics, model graphs, and hyperparameters.

**Features:**
- Real-time metric visualization
- Hyperparameter tracking
- Model graph visualization
- Histogram logging
- Image logging

**Configuration:**
```yaml
logging:
  tensorboard: true
  tensorboard_dir: data/tb
```

**Usage:**
```bash
# Start training with TensorBoard
hale-llm-train --config configs/diffusion/small.yaml

# View logs in browser
tensorboard --logdir data/tb
```

### 2. Weights & Biases (Cloud)
W&B provides cloud-based experiment tracking with advanced features like hyperparameter sweeps, model versioning, and team collaboration.

**Features:**
- Cloud-based experiment tracking
- Hyperparameter sweeps
- Model versioning
- Team collaboration
- Advanced visualizations
- Model gradient tracking

**Configuration:**
```yaml
logging:
  wandb: true
  wandb_project: "halodiffusion-llm"
  wandb_entity: "your-username-or-team"
  wandb_run_name: null  # Auto-generated if null
  wandb_tags: ["diffusion", "small"]
  wandb_watch_model: true  # Track gradients
  wandb_log_freq: 100  # Log frequency for gradients
```

**Setup:**
```bash
# Install W&B
pip install wandb

# Login (one-time setup)
wandb login

# Start training with W&B
hale-llm-train --config configs/diffusion/small.yaml
```

## Configuration Options

### TensorBoard Settings

| Parameter | Type | Default | Description |
|-----------|------|---------|-------------|
| `tensorboard` | bool | `true` | Enable TensorBoard logging |
| `tensorboard_dir` | str | `"data/tb"` | Directory for TensorBoard logs |

### Weights & Biases Settings

| Parameter | Type | Default | Description |
|-----------|------|---------|-------------|
| `wandb` | bool | `false` | Enable W&B logging |
| `wandb_project` | str | `null` | W&B project name (required if enabled) |
| `wandb_entity` | str | `null` | W&B username or team name |
| `wandb_run_name` | str | `null` | Custom run name (auto-generated if null) |
| `wandb_tags` | list[str] | `null` | Tags for organizing runs |
| `wandb_watch_model` | bool | `false` | Track model gradients and parameters |
| `wandb_log_freq` | int | `100` | Frequency for gradient logging |

## Example Configurations

### TensorBoard Only (Default)
```yaml
logging:
  level: INFO
  log_file: data/logs/hale_llm.log
  tensorboard: true
  tensorboard_dir: data/tb
  wandb: false
```

### W&B Only
```yaml
logging:
  level: INFO
  log_file: data/logs/hale_llm.log
  tensorboard: false
  wandb: true
  wandb_project: "halodiffusion-llm"
  wandb_entity: "my-team"
  wandb_tags: ["experiment", "v1"]
```

### Both TensorBoard and W&B
```yaml
logging:
  level: INFO
  log_file: data/logs/hale_llm.log
  tensorboard: true
  tensorboard_dir: data/tb
  wandb: true
  wandb_project: "halodiffusion-llm"
  wandb_entity: "my-team"
  wandb_run_name: "diffusion-small-exp1"
  wandb_tags: ["diffusion", "small", "baseline"]
  wandb_watch_model: true
  wandb_log_freq: 100
```

### W&B with Gradient Tracking
```yaml
logging:
  level: INFO
  tensorboard: true
  tensorboard_dir: data/tb
  wandb: true
  wandb_project: "halodiffusion-llm"
  wandb_watch_model: true  # Enable gradient tracking
  wandb_log_freq: 50  # Log gradients every 50 steps
```

## Logged Metrics

Both backends automatically log:

### Training Metrics
- `train/loss` - Training loss per step
- `train/lr` - Learning rate per step
- `train/grad_norm` - Gradient norm per step
- `train/epoch` - Current epoch
- `train/epoch_loss` - Average loss per epoch
- `train/final_loss` - Final training loss

### Evaluation Metrics
- `eval/loss` - Validation loss
- `eval/perplexity` - Model perplexity
- `eval/bits_per_token` - Bits per token
- `eval/token_accuracy` - Token-level accuracy
- `eval/masked_accuracy` - Masked token accuracy

### Model Metrics
- `model/n_params` - Total number of parameters

### Hyperparameters
- `variant` - Model variant
- `lr` - Learning rate
- `batch_size` - Batch size
- `d_model` - Model dimension
- `n_layers` - Number of layers
- `n_heads` - Number of attention heads
- `max_length` - Maximum sequence length
- `epochs` - Total epochs
- `steps` - Total steps
- `loss_type` - Loss function type
- `parallel_strategy` - Data parallelism strategy
- `world_size` - Number of distributed processes

## CLI Usage

### Override W&B Settings via CLI
```bash
# Enable W&B for a single run
hale-llm-train --config configs/diffusion/small.yaml \
  --wandb-project "my-project" \
  --wandb-entity "my-team"

# Disable logging
hale-llm-train --config configs/diffusion/small.yaml \
  --no-tensorboard \
  --no-wandb
```

## Distributed Training

In distributed training (DDP/FSDP), only the main process (rank 0) logs to TensorBoard and W&B to avoid conflicts and duplicate data.

```yaml
train:
  parallel_strategy: ddp  # or 'fsdp'

logging:
  tensorboard: true
  wandb: true
  wandb_project: "distributed-training"
```

## Best Practices

1. **Use TensorBoard for local development** - Fast, no internet required
2. **Use W&B for production experiments** - Better organization, team collaboration
3. **Enable both for important runs** - Redundancy and different visualization options
4. **Use tags in W&B** - Organize experiments by model type, dataset, etc.
5. **Enable gradient tracking sparingly** - `wandb_watch_model` adds overhead
6. **Set appropriate log frequency** - Balance between detail and performance

## Troubleshooting

### TensorBoard not showing data
```bash
# Check if logs are being written
ls -la data/tb/

# Ensure correct log directory
tensorboard --logdir data/tb
```

### W&B authentication issues
```bash
# Re-login
wandb login

# Check authentication
wandb verify
```

### W&B not installed
```bash
pip install wandb
```

### Gradient tracking causing OOM
```yaml
logging:
  wandb_watch_model: false  # Disable gradient tracking
```

## API Reference

See `src/hale_llm/utils/experiment_logger.py` for the full API:

```python
from hale_llm.utils.experiment_logger import make_experiment_logger

logger = make_experiment_logger(
    tensorboard_enabled=True,
    tensorboard_dir="runs",
    wandb_enabled=True,
    wandb_project="my-project",
)

# Log scalars
logger.log_scalars(step=100, metrics={"loss": 0.5, "accuracy": 0.9})

# Log hyperparameters
logger.log_hparams({"lr": 0.001, "batch_size": 32})

# Log text
logger.log_text("config", "model: transformer", step=0)

# Log images
logger.log_image("sample", image_tensor, step=100)

# Close logger
logger.close()
```

# Looped transformers (`arch: looped`)

A looped transformer reuses **one block of weights** for many passes. Depth becomes an iteration
count: `h_t = f_W(h_{t-1})`. Parameters scale with the block; compute scales with the number of
loops, which can change between training and inference.

Looped transformers come in two forms:

- **`variant: looped`** is a model package,
  [`src/hale_llm/models/models_looped/`](../src/hale_llm/models/models_looped/). It is a causal
  next-token LM on the looped backbone, laid out like every other paradigm:

  | File | Registers | What it holds |
  |---|---|---|
  | `model.py` | `LoopedLM` (`@register_variant("looped")`) and its collate | `forward_with_loops(batch, n)` and `last_loop_stats` |
  | `loss.py` | `@register_loss("looped")` | next-token NLL; the trainer adds the ACT ponder cost |
  | `sample.py` | `@register_sampler("looped")` | left-to-right decoding under a loop budget; `model.loops_per_token` records the passes per token |
  | `metrics.py` | `loop_scaling`, `ponder_histogram` | loss at `T  {1, T/2, T, 2T}`; the ACT passes-per-token distribution |

- **`arch: looped`** is a backbone. You can put it under any other `variant`, including masked
  diffusion, block diffusion, flow matching and MTP, with any `attn_type` and with diffusion time
  conditioning. Examples: `configs/looped/diffusion_nano.yaml` and `block_diffusion_nano.yaml`.

The building blocks live in HaleBlocks (`hale_core.nn.stacks.looped`, `hale_core.nn.layers.loop`),
so every Hale repo shares them.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  X["tokens"] --> EMB["embed + positions"]
  EMB --> P["prelude<br/>(distinct layers, once)"]
  P --> E(("e"))
  E --> INJ
  H0(("h = 0")) --> INJ["inject(h, e) + E"]
  INJ --> CORE["shared core block<br/>n_layers layers, weights W"]
  CORE -->|"t < T and not halted"| INJ
  CORE --> ACT{"ACT:   h<br/>(optional)"}
  ACT --> C["coda<br/>(distinct layers, once)"]
  C --> HEAD["LM head"]
```

## The model

| Piece | Config | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Shared core | `model.n_layers` | The block that is looped; its parameters are the only ones that grow with depth. |
| Loop count `T` | `loop.n_loops` | Number of passes. The compute depth is `prelude + T  n_layers + coda`. |
| Depth embedding `E` | `loop.depth_embedding` | `sinusoidal` (default, defined for any `t`), `learned` (one row per step) or `none`. This is the Universal Transformer's step coordinate, so the block can tell pass 1 from pass 5. |
| Input injection | `loop.input_injection` | `add`: `f(h + e)`; `concat`: `f(W[h; e])`, initialised to `h + e`; `none`: `h = e` and nothing re-enters. Injection keeps the input visible across long loops. |
| Prelude / coda | `loop.prelude_layers`, `loop.coda_layers` | Distinct layers before and after the loop, which map tokens into and out of the latent space. This is the recurrent-depth layout. |
| Sandwich norm | `loop.sandwich_norm` | Adds post-norms inside the core layers. It keeps `h_t` bounded, so the loop Jacobian `(I + J_f)` stays well conditioned. |

## Variants

### Fixed-T (Universal Transformer)

```yaml
model: {arch: looped, n_layers: 2, loop: {n_loops: 4}}
```

The simplest form: the same block runs `T` times, with depth embeddings. See
[`configs/looped/nano.yaml`](../configs/looped/nano.yaml). It has
the compute depth of `autoregressive/nano.yaml` with  of its transformer parameters.

### Adaptive Computation Time

```yaml
loop: {n_loops: 8, halting: act, act_threshold: 0.99, ponder_weight: 0.01}
```

**Halting.** Each token outputs a halting probability on every pass:

```
p = (wh + b)
```

A token halts as soon as ` p` would exceed `act_threshold`. Its output is `  h`, where
` = p` before the halting step and the remainder `R = 1   p` at it. Halted tokens are still
transformed, because other tokens attend to them, but their own output no longer changes. The
loop stops once every real token has halted. Padding and scratch tokens never vote.

**Ponder loss.** The trainer adds this to the variant's loss:

```
  mean(N(i) + R(i))
```

`` is `ponder_weight`, and larger values mean fewer passes. The trainer logs the terms as
`train/task_loss`, `train/ponder_loss`, `train/loop/mean_updates` and `train/loop/loops`. Each
variant's own loss function is unchanged.

**Initial bias.** `act_init_bias` is the initial halting logit. A negative value makes tokens
start by pondering longer. See
[`configs/looped/act.yaml`](../configs/looped/act.yaml).

### Recurrent depth and test-time compute

```yaml
loop:
  n_loops: 8
  min_loops: 2
  bptt_loops: 4
  prelude_layers: 1
  coda_layers: 1
  input_injection: concat
  exit_tolerance: 0.001
sample: {num_loops: 16}
```

**Training.** Two options make the model work at any loop count:

- `min_loops` samples `T ~ U{min_loops  n_loops}` for every step.
- `bptt_loops: k` backpropagates through the last `k` passes only. Earlier passes run without
  autograd, so activation memory is `O(k)` instead of `O(T)`. This is the one-step "phantom
  gradient" view of a DEQ.

**Inference.** The loop count is set per call:

- `sample.num_loops`, `hale-llm-sample --num-loops N` or
  `hale-llm-eval --num-loops N`;
- in Python, `hale_core.nn.stacks.loop_budget(model, n)`.

**Early exit.** `exit_tolerance` stops a pass once the largest per-token relative change satisfies
`|h_t  h_{t-1}| / |h_{t-1}| < tol`. This is fixed-point iteration: the loop runs until
`h* = f_W(h*)`, as in a DEQ. It is used at inference only.

Learned depth embeddings cannot run past `n_loops`; sinusoidal ones can. See
[`configs/looped/recurrent_depth.yaml`](../configs/looped/recurrent_depth.yaml).

### Scratchpad tokens

```yaml
loop: {scratch_tokens: 4}
```

These are learned memory slots, prepended for the stack only and dropped before the LM head. They
are carried and rewritten across loops as explicit working memory.

- **Causal or block-causal masks:** every token reads the slots, but the slots read only each
  other, so no future token leaks.
- **Bidirectional attention** (diffusion): the slots read and are read by the whole sequence.

See [`configs/looped/diffusion_nano.yaml`](../configs/looped/diffusion_nano.yaml).

## Training and evaluation

| Where | What |
|---|---|
| Trainer | `loss = task_loss +  auxiliary` (`hale_llm.losses.with_auxiliary_losses`); loop statistics are logged under `train/loop/*`. Gradient clipping (`train.grad_clip`) matters more than usual: backprop runs through `T` tied passes. |
| Eval metrics | `loop_depth` (any looped backbone) reports the mean `loops`, `mean_updates` and `ponder_cost`. With `variant: looped`, `loop_scaling` adds `loss@T1`, `loss@T{T/2}`, `loss@T{T}` and `loss@T{2T}`, the test-time scaling curve, and `ponder_histogram` adds the ACT `ponder_frac@k`, `ponder_mean`, `ponder_min` and `ponder_max`. |
| Sampling | Every registered sampler runs under the loop budget. |
| Checkpoints | The `model.loop` section is saved with the run config. `hale-looped` in HaleBlocks' LLM registry rebuilds a `variant: looped` checkpoint as a Hale-VLM / Hale-VLA language backbone. |

## Configs

| File | Paradigm | Loop |
|---|---|---|
| `configs/looped/nano.yaml` | looped | 2 layers  4 loops, add injection, sinusoidal depth |
| `configs/looped/small.yaml` | looped | 4 layers  4 loops (`d_model` 384): the compute depth of `autoregressive/small.yaml` |
| `configs/looped/act.yaml` | looped | ACT, up to 8 loops, ` = 0.01` |
| `configs/looped/recurrent_depth.yaml` | looped | prelude 1 + 2  U{2..8} + coda 1, BPTT through 4, 16 loops at inference, fixed-point exit |
| `configs/looped/diffusion_nano.yaml` | masked diffusion | 2  4 loops + 4 scratch tokens |
| `configs/looped/block_diffusion_nano.yaml` | block diffusion | 2  4 loops, block-causal |

```bash
hale-llm-train --config configs/looped/act.yaml
hale-llm-sample --config configs/looped/recurrent_depth.yaml --num-loops 32
hale-llm-eval --config configs/looped/act.yaml --metrics loss,perplexity,loop_scaling,ponder_histogram
```

## Python

```python
from hale_core.nn.backbones import build_backbone
from hale_core.nn.stacks import loop_aux_loss, loop_budget, loop_stats

lm = build_backbone(32000, arch="looped", d_model=512, n_heads=8, n_layers=2,
                    loop={"n_loops": 6, "halting": "act"})
logits = lm(ids)
loss = task_loss(logits) + loop_aux_loss(lm)      # ponder cost
print(loop_stats(lm))                             # {'loop/loops': 3.0, 'loop/mean_updates': 2.4, ...}
with loop_budget(lm.eval(), 12):                  # think longer at inference
    logits = lm(ids)
```

The stack alone, `LoopedStack`, runs on `(batch, seq, d_model)` hidden states, for a VLM or a
world model that brings its own embeddings.

## Trade-offs

| | Stacked transformer | Looped transformer |
|---|---|---|
| Parameters | `O(L  d)` | `O(d)`: one block |
| Backprop depth | `L` distinct layers | `T` tied passes (BPTT); `bptt_loops` bounds the memory |
| Compute per token | fixed | fixed (`T`), adaptive (ACT) or until convergence (`exit_tolerance`) |
| Gradients | separate per layer | every pass updates the same `W`; clip them |
| Latency | layers can be split across devices | passes are sequential |

## References

- Dehghani et al., *Universal Transformers*, ICLR 2019, arXiv:1807.03819.
- Graves, *Adaptive Computation Time for Recurrent Neural Networks*, arXiv:1603.08983.
- Bai, Kolter, Koltun, *Deep Equilibrium Models*, NeurIPS 2019, arXiv:1909.01377.
- Giannou et al., *Looped Transformers as Programmable Computers*, ICML 2023, arXiv:2301.13196.
- Yang et al., *Looped Transformers are Better at Learning Learning Algorithms*, ICLR 2024,
  arXiv:2311.12424.
- Geiping et al., *Scaling up Test-Time Compute with Latent Reasoning: A Recurrent Depth
  Approach*, 2025, arXiv:2502.05171.

# Online ("self") reinforcement learning

`hale_llm.rl` trains this repo's LLMs with **online GRPO**. The policy samples its own completions,
a reward scores them, rewards are normalised within each prompt's group, and clipped policy-gradient
steps follow. It works for the **autoregressive** and **masked-diffusion** variants and for
**verifiable** or **label-free self** rewards.

The step-by-step explanation, with diagrams, animations and runnable experiments, is in
[`notebooks/rl/01_online_rl.ipynb`](../notebooks/rl/01_online_rl.ipynb).

| | |
|---|---|
| ![one GRPO step](../assets/rl/grpo_step.gif) | ![policy evolution](../assets/rl/policy_evolution.gif) |
| ![self-reward](../assets/rl/policy_evolution_self_reward.gif) | ![d1 log-prob](../assets/rl/diffusion_log_prob.gif) |

## Quick start

```python
from hale_llm.rl import OnlineRLExperiment

exp = OnlineRLExperiment.from_yaml("configs/rl/arithmetic_autoregressive.yaml")
history = exp.run(exp.warm_start())          # ~30 s on a laptop CPU
print(history.evals[0], history.evals[-1])  # greedy accuracy before / after RL
```

Measured on the toy arithmetic task (seed 0, CPU), with numbers taken from the executed notebook:

| run (config) | accuracy before  after | unseen facts |
|---|---|---|
| GRPO, verifiable reward, AR (`arithmetic_autoregressive.yaml`, 1000 steps) | 20 %  94 % | 3 %  92 % |
| self-reward / majority vote, AR (`arithmetic_self_reward.yaml`, 600 steps) | 20 %  13 % | reward  0.9, accuracy falls |
| diffu-GRPO, masked diffusion (`arithmetic_diffusion.yaml`, 1500 steps) | 31 %  63 % | 7 %  43 % |

## Design

```mermaid
classDiagram
  class OnlineRLExperiment {
    +from_yaml(path, overrides)
    +warm_start() Module
    +run(policy, steps) RLHistory
  }
  class OnlineRLTrainer {
    +collect(prompts) RolloutBatch
    +update(batch) RLStepStats
    +step(prompts)
  }
  class RewardFunction {
    <<abstract>>
    +__call__(prompts, completions, G) Tensor
  }
  class PolicyLogProb {
    <<abstract>>
    +__call__(model, prompt_ids, completion_ids) Tensor
    +answer_distribution(model, prompt, candidates)
  }
  class GroupRelativeAdvantage
  class GRPOObjective
  OnlineRLExperiment --> OnlineRLTrainer
  OnlineRLExperiment --> OnlineRLConfig : YAML inherits
  OnlineRLTrainer --> RewardFunction
  OnlineRLTrainer --> PolicyLogProb
  OnlineRLTrainer --> GroupRelativeAdvantage
  OnlineRLTrainer --> GRPOObjective
  RewardFunction <|-- ExactMatchReward
  RewardFunction <|-- MajorityVoteReward
  PolicyLogProb <|-- AutoregressiveLogProb
  PolicyLogProb <|-- MaskedDiffusionLogProb
```

* **Registries:** `REWARDS` (`reward.type`) and `LOG_PROB_ESTIMATORS` (model `variant`). A new reward
  or model family is a new registered class; the trainer does not change.
* **Configs:** `configs/rl/base.yaml`, plus one-to-two-line children through `inherits:`.
  `OnlineRLConfig.from_yaml` resolves chains of any depth, rejects cycles and validates every field.
* **Diffusion:** `MaskedDiffusionLogProb` is d1's one-pass mean-field estimate (whole completion
  masked, about 15 % random prompt masking per inner epoch).
* **Animations:** `hale_llm.visualization.rl_films`, rendered from real runs.
* **Tests:** `tests/unit/test_online_rl.py`.

# Usage

```bash
uv sync
uv run hale-llm-train --config configs/block_diffusion/small.yaml --no-resume --viz
uv run hale-llm-sample --config configs/block_diffusion/small.yaml
uv run hale-llm-eval --config configs/block_diffusion/small.yaml
uv run hale-llm-viz --config configs/block_diffusion/small.yaml
tensorboard --logdir data/experiments
```

Configs inherit [`configs/base.yaml`](../configs/base.yaml). Full parameter list: [config.md](config.md).

## How it works

All four paradigms use the **same** transformer backbone, the same data (WikiText-2), and the same evaluation  only the *generation strategy* differs. This lets you measure each strategy fairly on equal footing.

```mermaid
flowchart TB
  BACK["Shared backbone\n(LGT transformer  384-d, 16 layers)\nSame weights  same data  same eval"]

  subgraph PARA["Four generation strategies  swap with one YAML key"]
    AR[" Autoregressive\nPredict one token at a time\nfrom left to right"]
    MD[" Masked diffusion\nStart with all blanks,\nunmask everything at once"]
    BD[" Block diffusion\nAR across chunks;\ndiffusion inside each chunk"]
    FM[" Flow matching\nShift token probabilities\nfrom uniform  real text"]
  end

  AR & MD & BD & FM --> BACK
```

> **The point:** by keeping everything else identical, any difference in quality comes only from the generation paradigm  not model size or data.

  <img src="assets/block_diffusion_step4900.gif" alt="Block diffusion decoding on WikiText  tokens unmask block by block" width="100%">
</p>

---

## Why this repo

The decoder is a small library: **components** (attention, FFN, layers), **transformer stacks** that run on hidden states `(B, S, D)` for a VLM or world model, and **token backbones** that map ids  logits. Masking, attention, FFN, and `arch` are config switches. Train, sample, eval, and visualize through one CLI. Details: [docs/core.md](docs/core.md).

| Switch | Options |
|---|---|
| Variant | `autoregressive`  `diffusion`  `block_diffusion`  `flow_matching`  `block_flow_matching`  `looped` ([docs/looped.md](docs/looped.md))  `mtp` (stub) |
| Attention mask | `causal`  `bidirectional`  `block_causal` |
| Attention impl | `mha`  `gqa`  `mqa` |
| Backbone | `transformer` (GPT-style)  `lgt` (local/global + dual RoPE)  `dit` (AdaLN-Zero)  `looped` (weight-tied recurrent depth + ACT, [docs/looped.md](docs/looped.md)) |
| FFN | `mlp`  `geglu`  DeepSeek-style `moe` |
| Token loss | `ce`  `focal` |

Shared core (`core/`, `dataloader/`, `training/`, `inference/`, `metrics/`) never imports a `models_*` package by name. Each paradigm lives in `src/hale_llm/models/models_<name>/` and registers with `@register_model` / `@register_loss` / `@register_sampler` / `@register_metric`.

---

## Quick start

```bash
uv sync
uv sync --extra dev
uv run pytest tests/unit -q
```

Train block diffusion on full WikiText-2 (MPS / CUDA via `device` in YAML):

```bash
uv run hale-llm-train --config configs/block_diffusion/small.yaml --no-resume --viz
```

Sample, eval, and write a GIF from `latest` or a named run:

```bash
uv run hale-llm-sample --config configs/block_diffusion/small.yaml
uv run hale-llm-eval --config configs/block_diffusion/small.yaml
uv run hale-llm-viz --config configs/block_diffusion/small.yaml
tensorboard --logdir data/experiments
```

---

## Variants

| Config | What it does |
|---|---|
| [`configs/autoregressive/small.yaml`](configs/autoregressive/small.yaml) | Next-token LM (reference) |
| [`configs/diffusion/small.yaml`](configs/diffusion/small.yaml) | Absorbing-state masked diffusion |
| [`configs/block_diffusion/small.yaml`](configs/block_diffusion/small.yaml) | AR across blocks, diffusion inside the block |
| [`configs/flow_matching/small.yaml`](configs/flow_matching/small.yaml) | Discrete flow matching |
| [`configs/block_flow_matching/small.yaml`](configs/block_flow_matching/small.yaml) | AR across blocks, discrete flow matching inside the block ([docs](docs/block_flow_matching.md)) |
| [`configs/looped/small.yaml`](configs/looped/small.yaml) | Looped transformer: one weight-tied block iterated T times (ACT, recurrent depth, test-time loops) |
| [`configs/mtp/small.yaml`](configs/mtp/small.yaml) | Multi-token heads (stub) |

Default small model: `d_model=384`, 16 layers, 8 heads, `d_ff=1536`, `max_length=128`, GPT-2 tokenizer, WikiText-2 with a **10-word sliding stride**. Diffusion, block diffusion, and flow matching use **LGT** (`arch: lgt`, local-global transformer). Autoregressive stays on `transformer`.

---

## Experiments

Each run is namespaced:

```
data/experiments/<variant>/<variant>_<YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS>/
  config.yaml          # resolved settings
  configs/             # source YAML chain
  checkpoints/last.pt
  logs/train.log
  tb/                  # TensorBoard
  viz/                 # generation GIFs
```

`latest` is a symlink to the newest folder. Resume with `--resume` (uses `latest`) or `--experiment NAME`.

Training GIFs write every `viz.every_n_steps` (default 50) using a short sampler. Prompts are taken from WikiText validation (`viz.prompt_source: dataset`). Override with `--prompt`.

---

## Configuration

YAML files under `configs/` are the source of truth. [`configs/base.yaml`](configs/base.yaml) holds shared size, data, and logging. Each variant file sets `inherits: ../base.yaml` and overrides only what that paradigm needs (`variant`, attention mask, `arch`, `block_size`).

Unknown keys are rejected. After a run, the merged file is saved as `data/experiments/<variant>/<run>/config.yaml`.

**High-level map**

| Section | Role |
|---|---|
| `variant` | Which model/loss/sampler (`autoregressive`, `diffusion`, `block_diffusion`, `flow_matching`, `block_flow_matching`, `looped`, `mtp`) |
| `model` | Width/depth, `arch` (`transformer` \| `lgt` \| `dit` \| `looped`, with `model.loop`), mask (`causal` \| `bidirectional` \| `block_causal`), FFN, time cond |
| `train` | `epochs` or `steps`, `batch_size`, `lr`, `loss_type`, resume |
| `data` | Hugging Face dataset (`Salesforce/wikitext` + `subset`) or `overfit_text`; `train_size: null` means the **full** split |
| `sample` / `eval` / `viz` | Generation length, metrics, GIF cadence |
| `device` | `mps`, `cuda`, or `cpu` |

**Typical edits**

```yaml
# WikiText-2, full split (null = no row cap)
data:
  dataset: Salesforce/wikitext
  subset: wikitext-2-raw-v1
  train_size: null

# Swap LGT for DiT on a denoiser
model:
  arch: dit

# Faster laptop run
train:
  batch_size: 8
  epochs: 2
data:
  train_size: 512
```

**Scaling out: Ray Train + WebDataset** (both off by default, `uv sync --extra distributed`)

```yaml
train:
  ray: {enabled: true, num_workers: 4, use_gpu: true}   # run on Ray workers with DDP, fault tolerant
data:
  webdataset: {enabled: true, root: data/webdataset}    # tokenise once -> tar shards -> stream
```

Full example: [`configs/examples/ray_webdataset.yaml`](configs/examples/ray_webdataset.yaml) 
details: [docs/distributed.md](docs/distributed.md).

Every key, type, default, and recipe: **[docs/config.md](docs/config.md)**.

---

## Adding a paradigm

1. New folder `src/hale_llm/models/models_<name>/` with model, loss, sampler (and optional metric).
2. One import in `src/hale_llm/models/__init__.py`.
3. One YAML under `configs/<name>/`.

Public facades stay the same: `train()`, `evaluate()`, `load_session()`.

---

## Deployment

Export any variant to TensorRT (data center, RTX, Jetson), MLX (Mac), AMD (MIGraphX, Ryzen AI
NPU) and Qualcomm (AI Hub, ExecuTorch QNN) with `hale-llm-deploy run configs/deploy/<variant>/<target>.yaml`.
Adapters, per-variant contracts, supported targets and research notes: [docs/deploy.md](docs/deploy.md).

## Tests

```bash
uv run pytest tests/unit -q
uv run pytest tests/integration/test_overfit_sanity.py -k autoregressive -v
```
